# Mode-diff debate, round 4: the sweep round

The user extended the budget from three exchanges to five, having been
asked at exhaustion. This is round 4 of 5. It is the SWEEP round; round 5
is the confirming round. So this is the round to be expansive in, and the
next one is where a dry result would mean something.

The tree has moved. New head is `9064753`, and the mirror was rebuilt at
the same path from it.

## What I changed from round 3

**Findings 1 and 2 together, because the fix is one thing.** The guard is
no longer an inline operand list. It is a function,
`Test-UnresolvableSpelling($label, $raw)`, returning `$null` when the
spelling is comparable and a refusal message when it is not. Every
operand calls it: the repo root, the mirror path, the override, each
extra input, and each discovered followed target.

I took your framing that the operand list was the defect rather than any
particular omission. It had been written twice, inline, and missed a
different operand each time.

Inside it:
- device prefixes `\\?\` and `\\.\`, on the whole string
- COLONS POSITIONALLY: the drive separator at index 1 is legitimate, any
  other colon names a stream. This replaces `Contains("::")`, which your
  `:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION` case walked through.
- trailing dot or space per component
- 8.3 short names, ANCHORED: `^[^.]{1,6}~[0-9]{1,6}$` or the same with a
  1-3 character extension.

**Finding 3** is filed as backlog item 98 rather than fixed, on your
recommendation. It records that you simulated the non-terminating error
and reached `New-Item`, and that a contaminated build was NOT reproduced
under real denial.

**Finding 4** now requires exit 1 and the specific
mirror-current-state diagnostic.

**Your regex counterexample was right and I fixed it in that direction
too.** `release~2026` is accepted now, and
`test_an_ordinary_name_holding_a_tilde_and_digits_is_accepted` is the
regression against re-tightening.

## Two errors of mine you should know about, because they bear on trust

**The helper shipped with a doubled backslash.** I generated it as
`.Replace("\\", "/")`, which PowerShell reads as a literal two-character
string, so the split never fired and every per-component check in the new
helper was DEAD CODE. A test caught it. This is the seventh
backslash-doubling incident on this branch.

**I wrote a test asserting a false conclusion** - that a stream-form repo
root was neutralized by `Resolve-Path` and built normally - inferred from
one earlier run that predated the guard working at all. It did not
survive execution.

I mention both because they are evidence about how much weight my
descriptions should carry. Verify the helper against the code.

Gate: 164 passed and 1 skipped under BOTH hosts, full `pytest evals` 2927
passed and 14 skipped, backlog lint clean, script pure ASCII.

## What I want from this round

Be expansive. This is the sweep.

1. **Attack `Test-UnresolvableSpelling` directly and hard.** It is the
   newest code and it has now been wrong twice. Specifically: is the
   colon rule right for a UNC path, where `\\server\share` has no drive
   letter at index 1? Is it right for a relative path? Does the anchored
   8.3 shape still catch every generated short name - what about names
   with more than 6 characters before the tilde, which Windows produces
   on collision, or `~10` and above?
2. **Is the operand coverage now complete?** Every path this tool writes
   to, deletes, copies to, or compares. If one is still uncovered, that
   is the fourth instance of this class and I want it named.
3. **Does calling the helper on followed targets break anything
   legitimate?** Those are discovered rather than supplied, so a refusal
   there rejects a tree the user did not spell wrong. If a legitimate
   repository with an ordinary junction now fails to mirror, that is a
   regression I introduced this round.
4. **The class sweep, widest yet.** Negative-only oracles, operand
   omissions, spelling-versus-identity comparisons, measurement
   overclaims, comments claiming more than the code, counts computed
   inside the documents they count, and dead code created by escaping
   errors. That last one is new to the list because I just shipped an
   instance of it.

+## 98. The mirror's own removal is unchecked, so a failed one builds over a stale tree
+Status: OPEN
+Cost: a build that fails to empty its destination copies over whatever survived, and the fingerprint then measures the resulting directory rather than proving it was freshly emptied, so a stale mirror can be certified as a fresh one
+Pairs: 95
+Verified: 2026-09-06 4b33bef11abd
+
+**Filed 2026-09-06 from the mode-diff debate for the identity window
+branch**, round 3, which asked whether refusing alias spellings was
+sufficient and was told plainly that it is not. Record:
+`docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/diff-debate.md`.
+
+`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` removes an existing mirror with
+`Remove-Item -LiteralPath $MirrorPath -Recurse -Force` and checks
+nothing afterwards. The script never sets `$ErrorActionPreference`, so a
+non-terminating failure - a locked file, a denied ACE, a handle held by
+another process - leaves the directory partly or wholly intact and
+execution continues to `New-Item` and then to the copy. The copy uses
+robocopy `/E`, which merges rather than replaces, so surviving files stay.
+
+The reviewer simulated a non-terminating error from that statement on
+both hosts and execution reached `New-Item`. It did NOT reproduce a
+complete contaminated build under real filesystem denial, so the end
+state is inferred from the control flow rather than observed, and that
+limit is stated rather than hidden.
+
+**Why the spelling guard is not the remedy.** The same round added
+refusals for device forms, stream syntax and 8.3 short names, and those
+close the ALIAS class. This is a different class: an ordinary path, an
+ordinary removal, and an ordinary failure. Refusing spellings does
+nothing for it, which is why it is filed rather than folded into that
+work.
+
+**What closing it means.** The removal terminates construction with a
+named error before anything is created or copied, and a test drives a
+real removal failure rather than a simulated one. The fingerprint's
+inability to distinguish a fresh directory from a merged one is the
+second half and may deserve its own treatment: it measures the result,
+which is exactly what a stale mirror also produces.

 # the spelling rather than reason about what each downstream call does
 # with it - the same decision, for the same reason, as the -ExtraInput
 # guard further down.
+# ONE HELPER, called by every operand, because the OPERAND LIST is what
+# keeps going wrong. This guard was written twice and each version named
+# its subjects inline: the first covered the repo root and the mirror
+# path and missed -OverrideOut, the second added the override and missed
+# the extra inputs and the followed link targets. Both gaps were walked
+# through by the diff reviewer on both hosts. A caller that has to
+# remember to add itself to a list is the defect; a function every path
+# calls is not.
+#
+# It returns $null when the spelling is one this tool can compare, and
+# the refusal message when it is not.
+function Test-UnresolvableSpelling($label, $raw) {
+    $s = [string]$raw
+    # DEVICE PREFIXES name the same directory under a spelling no
+    # comparison here can match.
+    if ($s.StartsWith("\\?\") -or $s.StartsWith("\\.\")) {
+        return ($label + " (" + $s + ") uses a device path form that this" +
+            " tool cannot resolve to the same spelling its comparisons" +
+            " use; pass an ordinary drive path")
+    }
+    # NTFS STREAM SYNTAX. The first version tested Contains("::") and the
+    # round-3 reviewer walked through it with
+    # `C:\path:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION`, whose colons are SEPARATED, which
+    # reported Directory on PowerShell 7 and reached the recursive delete.
+    # So the rule is positional instead: exactly one colon is legitimate,
+    # the drive separator at index 1, and any other colon is a stream.
+    $rest = $s
+    if ($rest.Length -ge 2 -and $rest[1] -eq ":" -and
+        [char]::IsLetter($rest[0])) {
+        $rest = $rest.Substring(2)
+    }
+    if ($rest.Contains(":")) {
+        return ($label + " (" + $s + ") contains a colon outside the drive" +
+            " separator, which names an NTFS stream rather than the file or" +
+            " directory this tool would compare; pass an ordinary path")
+    }
+    # ONE backslash. The generated form of this helper carried TWO,
+    # which PowerShell reads as a literal two-character string, so the
+    # split never fired and every component check below was dead. The
+    # short-name test caught it; nothing else would have.
+    foreach ($seg in $s.Replace("\", "/").Split("/")) {
+        if ($seg -match '[. ]$' -and $seg -ne "." -and $seg -ne "..") {
+            return ($label + " (" + $s + ") has a path component ending in" +
+                " a dot or a space, which Windows strips when it opens the" +
+                " path but PowerShell keeps in the string, so this tool" +
+                " cannot tell which directory it names; pass the exact name")
+        }
+        # 8.3 SHORT NAMES alias a long directory, and every comparison
+        # below is a string comparison, so an alias and its target compare
+        # unequal while naming one directory. Resolving one needs an open
+        # handle, which this tool will not take on a destination it is
+        # about to delete.
+        #
+        # THE SHAPE IS NARROW ON PURPOSE. The first version matched
+        # `~[0-9]+$` anywhere in a component and the round-3 reviewer
+        # showed it rejecting `release~2026`, an ordinary directory name,
+        # on both hosts - with a message telling the user to pass the full
+        # name when that already was the full name. A generated short name
+        # has at most six characters before the tilde and at most three of
+        # extension, so requiring that shape admits `release~2026` and
+        # still refuses `MULTI-~1` and `PXD1~1.SOU`.
+        if ($seg -match '^[^.]{1,6}~[0-9]{1,6}$' -or
+            $seg -match '^[^.]{1,6}~[0-9]{1,6}\.[^.]{1,3}$') {
+            return ($label + " (" + $s + ") has a component shaped like an" +
+                " 8.3 short name, which this tool cannot resolve to the" +
+                " long name its comparisons use. If that is the real name" +
+                " on disk, this tool does not support it: rename it or" +
+                " pass a path that does not go through it")
+        }
+    }
+    return $null
+}

478: function Get-FilesBeneath($start, $visited, $depth) {
479:     # Every file beneath $start, INCLUDING files behind every directory
480:     # link found under it, as full paths. Measured 2026-09-05 on both
481:     # hosts: Get-ChildItem -Recurse enters a link only when the link IS
482:     # the start path; from a parent it lists the link and does not pass
483:     # through it. So each link beneath a subject gets a listing of its
484:     # own, started at the link. $visited holds every resolved link
485:     # target this expansion has entered, seeded by the caller with the
486:     # repository root; a repeat is a refusal. Cross-vendor round 2
487:     # (2026-09-05) showed the visited set alone is not a cycle guard: a
488:     # RELATIVE symbolic-link target resolved against the link's spelled
489:     # parent yields a new string on every level of a cycle reached
490:     # through an outer junction. $depth is the bound that closes that:
491:     # a link nested more than 16 links deep is refused. Returns
492:     # @{Paths=..} or @{Error=..}.
493:     if ($depth -gt 16) {
494:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' sits more than 16 directory" +
495:             " links deep; the manifest refuses a link graph that deep" +
496:             " because a relative-link cycle presents exactly like it") }
497:     }
498:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
499:     $startAttr = 0
500:     try {
501:         $startAttr = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($start)
502:     } catch {
503:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be read: " +
504:                            $_.Exception.Message) }
505:     }
506:     if (($startAttr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0) {
507:         $target = $null
508:         try {
509:             $target = (Get-Item -LiteralPath $start -Force -ErrorAction Stop).Target
510:             if ($null -ne $target -and $target -isnot [string]) {
511:                 foreach ($candidate in $target) { $target = $candidate; break }
512:             }
513:         } catch { $target = $null }
514:         if ([string]::IsNullOrEmpty($target)) {
515:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' is a directory link whose" +
516:                                " target could not be read") }
517:         }
518:         $targetFull = ""
519:         # WRAPPED, because this script never sets $ErrorActionPreference
520:         # to Stop: a .NET exception here is NON-TERMINATING, so an
521:         # unwrapped GetFullPath left $targetFull empty, put the empty
522:         # string into the visited set, and returned Paths with no Error -
523:         # a failed measurement reading as a clean one. Measured by the
524:         # cross-vendor reviewer with a 270-character target
525:         # (PathTooLongException), 2026-09-05.
526:         try {
527:             if ([System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted($target)) {
528:                 $targetFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($target)
529:             } else {
530:                 $targetFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath(
531:                     [System.IO.Path]::Combine(
532:                         [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName(
533:                             [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($start)), $target))
534:             }
535:         } catch {
536:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' is a directory link whose" +
537:                 " target " + $target + " could not be resolved to a full" +
538:                 " path: " + $_.Exception.Message) }
539:         }
540:         if ($seamFailLinkTarget) {
541:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' link target resolution" +
542:                 " failed (test seam)") }
543:         }
544:         if (-not $visited.Add($targetFull.TrimEnd("\"))) {
545:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' reaches " + $targetFull +
546:                 " which this expansion already entered; the manifest refuses" +
547:                 " a link graph that repeats or cycles") }
548:         }
549:     }
550:     $found = $null
551:     try {
552:         $found = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $start -Recurse -File -Force `
553:             -ErrorAction Stop
554:     } catch {
555:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be enumerated: " +
556:                            $_.Exception.Message) }
557:     }
558:     foreach ($f in $found) { [void]$out.Add($f.FullName) }
559:     $links = $null
560:     try {
561:         $links = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $start -Recurse -Directory -Force `
562:             -ErrorAction Stop | Where-Object {
563:                 ($_.Attributes -band [System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0 })
564:     } catch {
565:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be enumerated for" +
566:                            " links: " + $_.Exception.Message) }
567:     }
568:     foreach ($l in $links) {
569:         $inner = Get-FilesBeneath $l.FullName $visited ($depth + 1)
570:         if ($inner.ContainsKey("Error")) { return $inner }
571:         foreach ($p in @($inner.Paths)) { [void]$out.Add($p) }
572:     }
573:     return @{ Paths = @($out) }
574: }

576: function Get-ContentManifest($repo, $paths) {
577:     # Directories expand RECURSIVELY to their files: a directory subject
578:     # such as References/ is never one manifest entry, because a hash over
579:     # a directory name identifies nothing.
580:     # A directory link beneath a subject is expanded by a listing started
581:     # at the link (Get-FilesBeneath), because a recursion from the parent
582:     # does not pass through it on either host.
583:     $files = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
584:     foreach ($p in $paths) {
585:         $full = Join-Path $repo $p
586:         if (Test-Path $full -PathType Container) {
587:             # -Stop, never the default: a swallowed enumeration error
588:             # omits every file under an unreadable subdirectory and the
589:             # manifest then reads as coverage of a tree it never saw.
590:             # Seeded with the repository root: a link back onto the root
591:             # is a cycle on its first step, not its second.
592:             $visited = New-Object "System.Collections.Generic.HashSet[string]" (
593:                 [System.StringComparer]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)
594:             [void]$visited.Add([System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($repo).TrimEnd("\"))
595:             $beneath = Get-FilesBeneath $full $visited 0
596:             if ($beneath.ContainsKey("Error")) {
597:                 return @{ Error = ("'" + $p + "': " + $beneath.Error) }
598:             }
599:             foreach ($fullName in @($beneath.Paths)) {
600:                 $rel = $fullName.Substring($repo.Length + 1)
601:                 [void]$files.Add($rel.Replace("\", "/"))
602:             }
603:         } elseif (Test-Path $full -PathType Leaf) {
604:             [void]$files.Add($p.TrimEnd("/").Replace("\", "/"))
605:         } else {
606:             # A baseline path with nothing behind it is a stop. Skipping it
607:             # would leave a hole in the manifest that reads as coverage.
608:             return @{ Error = "baseline path '$p' has no file behind it" }
609:         }
610:     }
611:     $unique = [string[]]@($files | Sort-Object -Unique)
612:     [Array]::Sort($unique, [System.StringComparer]::Ordinal)
613:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
614:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
615:     foreach ($rel in $unique) {
616:         # A failed ReadAllBytes is NON-TERMINATING on both hosts
617:         # (measured 2026-08-04), so without this catch $bytes keeps the
618:         # PREVIOUS file's contents and this line records that hash for
619:         # the file it could not read - deterministically, so the wrong
620:         # value reproduces on every later run. Evidence that quietly
621:         # names the wrong bytes is worse than no evidence.
622:         $bytes = $null
623:         try {
624:             $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Join-Path $repo $rel))
625:         } catch {
626:             $sha.Dispose()
627:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $rel + "' could not be read: " +
628:                                $_.Exception.Message) }
629:         }
630:         $hex = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString($sha.ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
631:         [void]$out.Add($rel + " " + $hex)
632:     }
633:     return @{ Paths = @($out) }
634: }

1297: function Test-UnresolvableSpelling($label, $raw) {
1298:     $s = [string]$raw
1299:     # DEVICE PREFIXES name the same directory under a spelling no
1300:     # comparison here can match.
1301:     if ($s.StartsWith("\\?\") -or $s.StartsWith("\\.\")) {
1302:         return ($label + " (" + $s + ") uses a device path form that this" +
1303:             " tool cannot resolve to the same spelling its comparisons" +
1304:             " use; pass an ordinary drive path")
1305:     }
1306:     # NTFS STREAM SYNTAX. The first version tested Contains("::") and the
1307:     # round-3 reviewer walked through it with
1308:     # `C:\path:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION`, whose colons are SEPARATED, which
1309:     # reported Directory on PowerShell 7 and reached the recursive delete.
1310:     # So the rule is positional instead: exactly one colon is legitimate,
1311:     # the drive separator at index 1, and any other colon is a stream.
1312:     $rest = $s
1313:     if ($rest.Length -ge 2 -and $rest[1] -eq ":" -and
1314:         [char]::IsLetter($rest[0])) {
1315:         $rest = $rest.Substring(2)
1316:     }
1317:     if ($rest.Contains(":")) {
1318:         return ($label + " (" + $s + ") contains a colon outside the drive" +
1319:             " separator, which names an NTFS stream rather than the file or" +
1320:             " directory this tool would compare; pass an ordinary path")
1321:     }
1322:     # ONE backslash. The generated form of this helper carried TWO,
1323:     # which PowerShell reads as a literal two-character string, so the
1324:     # split never fired and every component check below was dead. The
1325:     # short-name test caught it; nothing else would have.
1326:     foreach ($seg in $s.Replace("\", "/").Split("/")) {
1327:         if ($seg -match '[. ]$' -and $seg -ne "." -and $seg -ne "..") {
1328:             return ($label + " (" + $s + ") has a path component ending in" +
1329:                 " a dot or a space, which Windows strips when it opens the" +
1330:                 " path but PowerShell keeps in the string, so this tool" +
1331:                 " cannot tell which directory it names; pass the exact name")
1332:         }
1333:         # 8.3 SHORT NAMES alias a long directory, and every comparison
1334:         # below is a string comparison, so an alias and its target compare
1335:         # unequal while naming one directory. Resolving one needs an open
1336:         # handle, which this tool will not take on a destination it is
1337:         # about to delete.
1338:         #
1339:         # THE SHAPE IS NARROW ON PURPOSE. The first version matched
1340:         # `~[0-9]+$` anywhere in a component and the round-3 reviewer
1341:         # showed it rejecting `release~2026`, an ordinary directory name,
1342:         # on both hosts - with a message telling the user to pass the full
1343:         # name when that already was the full name. A generated short name
1344:         # has at most six characters before the tilde and at most three of
1345:         # extension, so requiring that shape admits `release~2026` and
1346:         # still refuses `MULTI-~1` and `PXD1~1.SOU`.
1347:         if ($seg -match '^[^.]{1,6}~[0-9]{1,6}$' -or
1348:             $seg -match '^[^.]{1,6}~[0-9]{1,6}\.[^.]{1,3}$') {
1349:             return ($label + " (" + $s + ") has a component shaped like an" +
1350:                 " 8.3 short name, which this tool cannot resolve to the" +
1351:                 " long name its comparisons use. If that is the real name" +
1352:                 " on disk, this tool does not support it: rename it or" +
1353:                 " pass a path that does not go through it")
1354:         }
1355:     }
1356:     return $null
1357: }

1825:             " review input must not be a file this tool overwrites")
1826:         exit 2
1827:     }
1828:     # SPELLING EQUALITY IS NOT IDENTITY, and the comparison above is only
1829:     # spelling. With C:\alias a junction to C:\out, a mirror at
1830:     # C:\out\mirror and an extra input at
1831:     # C:\alias\mirror.source-manifest, BOTH output paths have ordinary
1832:     # ancestors and pass their own alias checks, while the extra input
1833:     # keeps the alias spelling and compares unequal - and -Force would
1834:     # then remove the very file that extra input names, after which the
1835:     # unchecked Copy-Item can leave it out of the mirror entirely. This
1836:     # tool cannot establish physical identity here, so it REFUSES the
1837:     # case it cannot decide rather than guessing.
1838:     # A TRAILING DOT OR SPACE ON ANY COMPONENT defeats the comparison
1839:     # above. Windows strips it when it OPENS the file; PowerShell keeps
1840:     # it in the path. Measured 2026-09-05: `BACKLOG.md.` passes
1841:     # Test-Path, resolves WITH the dot, hashes identical to
1842:     # `BACKLOG.md`, and compares unequal - so an extra input spelled
1843:     # `<mirror>.source-manifest.` passes this guard and still names the
1844:     # file -Force is about to remove. Refuse the spelling.
1845:     foreach ($seg in $eiNorm.Split('/')) {
1846:         if ($seg -match '[. ]$') {
1847:             Write-Output ("ERROR: -ExtraInput '" + $eiResolved + "' has" +
1848:                 " a path component ending in a dot or a space, which" +
1849:                 " Windows strips when it opens the file but PowerShell" +
1850:                 " keeps in the path, so this tool cannot tell which file" +
1851:                 " it names; pass the exact name")
1852:             exit 2
1853:         }
1854:     }
1855:     $eiHit = Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink $eiResolved
1856:     if ($eiHit) {
1857:         Write-Output ("ERROR: -ExtraInput '" + $eiResolved + "' is reached" +
1858:             " through a directory link at " + $eiHit + ", so this tool" +
1859:             " cannot tell whether it names the same file as the source" +
1860:             " manifest it replaces; pass the path behind the link")
1861:         exit 2
1862:     }
1863: }
1864: 
1865: # LAST, because a build that is going to be refused must not have deleted
1866: # anything first. Read the ATTRIBUTES rather than calling Test-Path,
1867: # because the Directory bit and the not-there case have to be told apart
1868: # in one read, and this is the one place where a wrong "it is not there"
1869: # turns into a write.
1870: #

1536: # Every directory link target this walk has already followed. Case
1707: # target lives outside it. Cross-vendor round 1 (2026-09-05) named the

1717: function Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink($path) {
1718:     # The path itself, then each existing ancestor up to the drive root:
1719:     # is any of them a reparse point? Attributes are read directly
1720:     # rather than after a Test-Path, because a DANGLING junction is a
1721:     # reparse point Test-Path may report as absent; a missing entry is
1722:     # the one condition that skips a level, and it is recognised by the
1723:     # exception type, never by a false from a helper. Returns the
1724:     # offending path or $null.
1725:     $probe = ([string]$path).TrimEnd("\", "/")
1726:     while ($probe -and -not [string]::IsNullOrEmpty([System.IO.Path]::GetFileName($probe))) {
1727:         $pa = 0
1728:         $missing = $false
1729:         try {
1730:             $pa = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($probe)
1731:         } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
1732:             $missing = $true
1733:         } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
1734:             $missing = $true
1735:         } catch {
1736:             Write-Output ("ERROR: " + $probe + " could not be read while" +
1737:                 " checking for a directory link: " + $_.Exception.Message)
1738:             exit 2
1739:         }
1740:         if (-not $missing -and (($pa -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0)) {
1741:             return $probe
1742:         }
1743:         $probe = [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName($probe)
1744:     }
1745:     return $null
1746: }

4262: ## 98. The mirror's own removal is unchecked, so a failed one builds over a stale tree
4263: Status: OPEN
4264: Cost: a build that fails to empty its destination copies over whatever survived, and the fingerprint then measures the resulting directory rather than proving it was freshly emptied, so a stale mirror can be certified as a fresh one
4265: Pairs: 95
4266: Verified: 2026-09-06 4b33bef11abd
4267: 
4268: **Filed 2026-09-06 from the mode-diff debate for the identity window
4269: branch**, round 3, which asked whether refusing alias spellings was
4270: sufficient and was told plainly that it is not. Record:
4271: `docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/diff-debate.md`.
4272: 
4273: `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` removes an existing mirror with
4274: `Remove-Item -LiteralPath $MirrorPath -Recurse -Force` and checks
4275: nothing afterwards. The script never sets `$ErrorActionPreference`, so a
4276: non-terminating failure - a locked file, a denied ACE, a handle held by
4277: another process - leaves the directory partly or wholly intact and
4278: execution continues to `New-Item` and then to the copy. The copy uses
4279: robocopy `/E`, which merges rather than replaces, so surviving files stay.
4280: 
4281: The reviewer simulated a non-terminating error from that statement on
4282: both hosts and execution reached `New-Item`. It did NOT reproduce a
4283: complete contaminated build under real filesystem denial, so the end
4284: state is inferred from the control flow rather than observed, and that
4285: limit is stated rather than hidden.
4286: 
4287: **Why the spelling guard is not the remedy.** The same round added
4288: refusals for device forms, stream syntax and 8.3 short names, and those
4289: close the ALIAS class. This is a different class: an ordinary path, an
4290: ordinary removal, and an ordinary failure. Refusing spellings does
4291: nothing for it, which is why it is filed rather than folded into that
4292: work.
4293: 
4294: **What closing it means.** The removal terminates construction with a
4295: named error before anything is created or copied, and a test drives a
4296: real removal failure rather than a simulated one. The fingerprint's
4297: inability to distinguish a fresh directory from a merged one is the
4298: second half and may deserve its own treatment: it measures the result,
4299: which is exactly what a stale mirror also produces.

38: Wrapper exit 0, `reply-present`. Route verified from the transcript

40: high`, `sandbox: read-only`, `workdir: C:\Temp\pxd1`. Binder verdict
41: `clean`, `sealed: sealed`, so the reply is bound to the brief this side
42: sent.

44: Claim 1 SURVIVED: the reviewer traced the reader, the size refusal, the
45: parser, the renderer and the exception fallback, and found no path by
46: which sidecar content changes the verdict.

48: Five findings, all CONFIRMED, all applied.
49: 
50: **1. The overlap guard compares spelling, and a trailing dot defeats it.**
51: The one that matters. `-MirrorPath <repo>.` passes the equal, inside and
52: contains tests because they are string comparisons, while Windows strips
53: the trailing dot when it OPENS the path. The reviewer executed the
54: shipped construction prefix on both hosts with `Remove-Item` replaced by
55: an interceptor and reached the recursive deletion call.
56: 
57: **Its stated consequence is contested, and this side measured it.** The
58: reviewer reported the bypass as reaching recursive deletion of the
59: source. Measured 2026-09-05 on BOTH hosts: `Remove-Item` on a
60: trailing-dot path throws `PSArgumentException` and deletes NOTHING, on
61: `-LiteralPath` and `-Path` alike, and the victim survives. The reviewer's
62: interceptor made the deletion unobservable, so what its probe established
63: is REACHABILITY, not deletion, and the round-1 wording claims the latter.
64: 
65: That does not save the guard. The failed removal is NON-TERMINATING, this
66: script never sets `$ErrorActionPreference`, so the build carries on and
67: constructs a mirror at a path naming the tree under review. The fix is
68: the same either way and is the one the reviewer asked for: refuse the
69: spelling before any comparison, on BOTH operands, since both are
70: user-supplied. Two tests, one per operand, and the mirror-path test
71: asserts the source survives.
72: 
73: The defect is PRE-EXISTING, not introduced here. It is the same class the
74: branch already closed for `-ExtraInput`, left open on the destructive
75: path - a fix that did not sweep its own class.
76: 
77: **2. Two oracles go green without measuring anything.** The
78: control-character test and the trailing-newline test assert only an exit
79: code and the ABSENCE of a string. The reader's own failure fallback
80: prints `what moved: unknown` and satisfies both, so each passes when the
81: behaviour it names was never exercised. Both now assert the whole
82: rendered name positively and that `unknown` is absent. The substring form
83: also accepted a doubled escape prefix, which is exactly how the shipped
84: renderer emitted two backslashes through four rounds without a test
85: noticing.
86: 
87: **3. The item 93 amendment outran its evidence.** Written as "item 90 did
88: not close a gap" and "whatever produced it is not a property of the
89: tracked tree". Three fresh runs that do not hit whatever conditions
90: produced 18m42s exclude nothing about the code. Reworded in `BACKLOG.md`
91: and `gate-results.md` to say only that it did not reproduce, and that the
92: cause is unknown with no candidate favoured.
93: 
94: **4. The record claimed static review COULD NOT have caught the oracle
95: defects.** It said so on the grounds that no round ran the tests, in a
96: paragraph immediately followed by an explanation of a phrase mismatch
97: that is plain to read without executing anything. Discovery history is
98: not proof that earlier discovery was impossible. Reworded to "the reviews
99: missed them; implementation exposed them".
100: 
101: **5. A stale parser comment** still taught the trailing-newline
102: explanation that the implementation comment ten lines below it retracts.

142: Three residual findings, all CONFIRMED, all applied.
143: 
144: **1. The new guard still admitted three aliases.** The deeper point, and
145: the reviewer put it exactly right: rejecting trailing punctuation does
146: not establish filesystem identity, and every comparison in this tool is a
147: string comparison. Reachability demonstrated on both hosts, deletion
148: intercepted:
149: 
150: | source | mirror | reached delete on |
151: | --- | --- | --- |
152: | `<repo>\skills\multi-model-verify` | `<repo>\skills\MULTI-~1` | both hosts |
153: | `<repo>::$INDEX_ALLOCATION` | `<repo>` | PowerShell 7 |
154: | `<repo>` | `\\?\<repo>` | Windows PowerShell 5.1 |
155: 
156: It also confirmed with `Remove-Item -WhatIf` that the 8.3 short form
157: names the long directory on both hosts.
158: 
159: The fix keeps this tool's existing stance rather than inventing a new
160: one: it REFUSES the spellings it cannot resolve. Device forms `\\?\`
161: and `\\.\`, NTFS stream syntax `::`, and 8.3 short-name components are
162: each refused with their own message. Resolving an alias to filesystem
163: identity needs an open handle, and this tool will not take one on a
164: destination it is about to delete.
165: 
166: **2. The guard shipped with the override operand missing.** An
167: `-OverrideOut` of `<repo>.\override.txt` passed every check and named a
168: location inside the tree under review, which the build then hands to the
169: probe's writer. This is finding 1 of round 1 on a third operand: the
170: first version of the guard covered two operands and stopped. Guarding
171: operands one at a time is how the class survived being fixed once
172: already, so the fix builds a subject list and the test says why.
173: 
174: **3. A third negative-only oracle.**
175: `test_an_unmeasurable_expected_digest_is_refused` asserted only
176: `returncode != 0`, which exit 2 with empty stdout satisfies. This
177: script's contract separates 1, blocked with a reason, from 2, a script or
178: environment error, so that oracle could not tell a working refusal from a
179: crash in the code meant to refuse. Now requires exit 1 and the named
180: diagnostic.
181: 
182: **What the sweep covered and what it could not.** Path aliases, omitted
183: guard operands, negative-only assertions and their helpers, amended
184: measurement conclusions, adjacent comments. It found no further
185: measurement overclaim and no self-count defect. Two stated limits, both
186: the reviewer's own: live UNC access was unavailable, so the guard's
187: component split is unverified end to end for UNC, and it did not re-run
188: the full pytest suite.

192: ## Round 3 (`Astra D3`), and the budget is spent
193: 
194: Resumed, route verified, binder `clean` and `sealed`. Mirror rebuilt at
195: the same path from head `d80f0bc`.
196: 
197: **NOT A DRY ROUND.** Four more confirmed findings, all applied. Round 3
198: was dispatched as the confirming round of a declared budget of three, and
199: the debate therefore CANNOT terminate here on its own terms:
200: `debate-protocol.md` allows termination only on an adjudicated dry round.
201: 
202: **1. The colon rule was wrong.** `Contains("::")` catches an unnamed
203: stream and misses `<path>:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION`, whose colons are
204: SEPARATED. On PowerShell 7 that path reports `Directory` and reached the
205: intercepted delete. The rule is positional now: one colon is legitimate,
206: the drive separator at index 1, and any other colon names a stream.
207: 
208: **2. The guard was still missing two operand sets**, and this is the
209: third time the same shape has been found. `ExtraInputPaths` and the
210: discovered `followedTargets` were both outside it. The reviewer supplied
211: THIS MIRROR'S OWN SIDECAR under its real short alias
212: `C:\Temp\PXD1~1.SOU` as an extra input; it passed
213: every check and reached the removal of
214: `C:\Temp\pxd1.source-manifest`, after which the
215: unchecked copy would leave a declared review input out of a mirror the
216: digest certifies.
217: 
218: The fix is not another operand. It is ONE HELPER,
219: `Test-UnresolvableSpelling`, that every operand calls. Naming subjects
220: inline is what failed twice; a caller that has to remember to add itself
221: to a list is the defect.
222: 
223: **3. The unchecked removal is filed as item 98**, on the reviewer's own
224: recommendation that the spelling work is not its remedy. Different class:
225: an ordinary path, an ordinary removal, an ordinary failure. It simulated
226: a non-terminating error on both hosts and reached `New-Item`; it did NOT
227: reproduce a contaminated build under real denial, and item 98 says so.
228: 
229: **4. A fourth negative-only oracle**,
230: `test_a_mirror_whose_current_state_cannot_be_measured_is_refused`,
231: directly beside the third. Accepted any nonzero exit and the generic
232: substring `could not be`, so changing the branch to exit 2 would have
233: left it green.
234: 
235: **The reviewer also refuted the guard in the other direction, which is
236: what it was asked to do.** The first 8.3 pattern matched `~[0-9]+$`
237: anywhere in a component, so it rejected `release~2026` - an ordinary
238: directory name - on both hosts, with a message telling the user to pass
239: the full name when that already was the full name. The shape is anchored
240: now, and `test_an_ordinary_name_holding_a_tilde_and_digits_is_accepted`
241: is the regression against tightening it back.
242: 
243: ## Two errors of this side's own, both found by running things
244: 
245: **The helper shipped with a doubled backslash.** Generated as
246: `.Replace("\\", "/")`, which PowerShell reads as a literal
247: two-character string, so the split never fired and EVERY component check
248: in the new helper was dead code. The short-name test caught it; nothing
249: else would have. Seventh backslash-doubling incident on this branch.
250: 
251: **A test asserted the wrong conclusion and did not survive execution.** A
252: draft of `test_a_stream_form_repo_root_is_refused_too` claimed the stream
253: spelling was neutralized by `Resolve-Path` and the build proceeded
254: normally, inferred from one earlier run that predated the guard working
255: at all. It is refused. The test now records which mechanism refuses it.

259: still pure ASCII.

3053:     oracle that accepts either cannot tell a working refusal from a crash
3054:     in the code that was supposed to refuse.
3055: 
3056:     Found by the round-2 diff reviewer as a third instance of the
3057:     negative-only oracle class.
3058:     """
3059:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
3060:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
3061:     _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
3062:     proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident, mirror_state_sha256="")
3063:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
3064:     assert "recorded mirror state hash is missing or malformed" in proc.stdout, (
3065:         proc.stdout)
3066: 
3067: 
3068: def test_a_mirror_whose_current_state_cannot_be_measured_is_refused(tmp_path):
3069:     # Not the same case as the one above: that one supplies a bad
3070:     # EXPECTED value, and this one makes the LIVE measurement itself
3071:     # fail - the direction that could otherwise read as clean. Mirrors
3072:     # the source-side version of this case (icacls deny, same shape).
3073:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
3074:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
3075:     _, ident = build_and_read(repo, mirror)
3076:     denied = mirror / "untracked.txt"
3077:     user = os.environ.get("USERNAME") or ""
3078:     if not user:
3079:         pytest.skip("USERNAME not set; cannot build a deny ACE")
3080:     deny = subprocess.run(["icacls", str(denied), "/deny", user + ":(RD)"],
3081:                           capture_output=True, text=True)
3082:     if deny.returncode != 0:
3083:         pytest.skip("icacls deny unavailable: " + deny.stdout + deny.stderr)
3084:     try:
3085:         proc = run_verify(repo, mirror, ident)
3086:         # EXIT 1 AND THE NAMED DIAGNOSTIC. `!= 0` plus the generic
3087:         # substring "could not be" left this green if the branch were
3088:         # changed to exit 2, which is this script's code for an error in
3089:         # the tool rather than a block it decided on. Third instance of
3090:         # the negative-only oracle class, found by the round-3 reviewer
3091:         # next door to the second.
3092:         assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
3093:         assert "the mirror's current state could not be measured" in proc.stdout, (
3094:             proc.stdout)
3095:     finally:
3096:         undo = subprocess.run(["icacls", str(denied), "/remove:d", user],
3097:                               capture_output=True, text=True)
3098:         assert undo.returncode == 0, undo.stdout + undo.stderr
3099:         assert denied.read_text(), "the deny ACE is still in force"
3100: 
diff-astra-r1-reply.md
diff-astra-r1-transcript.txt
187: component split is unverified end to end for UNC, and it did not re-run
188: the full pytest suite.
189: 
190: Verdict: **FIX**.
191: 
192: ## Round 3 (`Astra D3`), and the budget is spent
193: 
194: Resumed, route verified, binder `clean` and `sealed`. Mirror rebuilt at
195: the same path from head `d80f0bc`.
196: 
197: **NOT A DRY ROUND.** Four more confirmed findings, all applied. Round 3
198: was dispatched as the confirming round of a declared budget of three, and
199: the debate therefore CANNOT terminate here on its own terms:
200: `debate-protocol.md` allows termination only on an adjudicated dry round.
201: 
202: **1. The colon rule was wrong.** `Contains("::")` catches an unnamed
203: stream and misses `<path>:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION`, whose colons are
204: SEPARATED. On PowerShell 7 that path reports `Directory` and reached the
205: intercepted delete. The rule is positional now: one colon is legitimate,
206: the drive separator at index 1, and any other colon names a stream.
207: 
208: **2. The guard was still missing two operand sets**, and this is the
209: third time the same shape has been found. `ExtraInputPaths` and the
210: discovered `followedTargets` were both outside it. The reviewer supplied
211: THIS MIRROR'S OWN SIDECAR under its real short alias
212: `C:\Temp\PXD1~1.SOU` as an extra input; it passed
213: every check and reached the removal of
214: `C:\Temp\pxd1.source-manifest`, after which the
215: unchecked copy would leave a declared review input out of a mirror the
216: digest certifies.
217: 
218: The fix is not another operand. It is ONE HELPER,
219: `Test-UnresolvableSpelling`, that every operand calls. Naming subjects
220: inline is what failed twice; a caller that has to remember to add itself
221: to a list is the defect.
222: 
223: **3. The unchecked removal is filed as item 98**, on the reviewer's own
224: recommendation that the spelling work is not its remedy. Different class:
225: an ordinary path, an ordinary removal, an ordinary failure. It simulated
226: a non-terminating error on both hosts and reached `New-Item`; it did NOT
227: reproduce a contaminated build under real denial, and item 98 says so.
228: 
229: **4. A fourth negative-only oracle**,
230: `test_a_mirror_whose_current_state_cannot_be_measured_is_refused`,
231: directly beside the third. Accepted any nonzero exit and the generic
232: substring `could not be`, so changing the branch to exit 2 would have
233: left it green.
234: 
235: **The reviewer also refuted the guard in the other direction, which is
236: what it was asked to do.** The first 8.3 pattern matched `~[0-9]+$`
237: anywhere in a component, so it rejected `release~2026` - an ordinary
238: directory name - on both hosts, with a message telling the user to pass
239: the full name when that already was the full name. The shape is anchored
240: now, and `test_an_ordinary_name_holding_a_tilde_and_digits_is_accepted`
241: is the regression against tightening it back.
242: 
243: ## Two errors of this side's own, both found by running things
244: 
245: **The helper shipped with a doubled backslash.** Generated as
246: `.Replace("\\", "/")`, which PowerShell reads as a literal
247: two-character string, so the split never fired and EVERY component check
248: in the new helper was dead code. The short-name test caught it; nothing
249: else would have. Seventh backslash-doubling incident on this branch.
250: 
251: **A test asserted the wrong conclusion and did not survive execution.** A
252: draft of `test_a_stream_form_repo_root_is_refused_too` claimed the stream
253: spelling was neutralized by `Resolve-Path` and the build proceeded
254: normally, inferred from one earlier run that predated the guard working
255: at all. It is refused. The test now records which mechanism refuses it.
256: 
257: Gate after the fixes: 164 passed and 1 skipped under BOTH hosts, full
258: `pytest evals` 2927 passed and 14 skipped, backlog lint clean, script
259: still pure ASCII.

1. **CONFIRMED — the source root’s ancestors remain outside the alias guard.**  
   The script checks whether the source root **itself** is a reparse point, but checks ancestors only for the mirror, override, sidecar, extra inputs, and followed targets. A junction above an otherwise ordinary source directory therefore escapes protection. ([Source-root check](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1519), [output ancestor checks](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1748), [target check](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1777), [extra-input check](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1855))

   On **both hosts**, I executed the construction prefix with `Remove-Item` intercepted, using these paths through the existing `My Documents` junction:

   - RepoRoot: `C:\Users\Brandon\My Documents\parallax\skills`
   - MirrorPath: `C:\Users\Brandon\Documents\parallax\skills`

   Both reached the recursive-removal statement against the second path. The lexical overlap comparison accepts these different strings. **This proves unsafe reachability; I did not execute removal or demonstrate deletion.** ([Overlap comparison](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1378), [removal](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1918))

   **Change:** apply `Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink` to RepoRoot before destructive operations, with a regression placing the junction above the source root. The spelling helper covers this operand; the separate alias guard does not.

2. **CONFIRMED — the anchored regex still rejects names that cannot be 8.3 aliases.**  
   The pattern independently permits six characters before the tilde and six digits after it. On both hosts, the helper refused `backup~2026`, `ABCDEF~123456`, and `a b~1`. These violate the eight-character basename limit or the prohibition on spaces in an 8.3 name. ([Regex](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1347), [Microsoft’s 8.3 specification](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-fscc/18e63b13-ba43-4f5f-a5b7-11e871b71f14))

   Calling this helper on discovered targets extends that false refusal to an ordinary junction whose real target has one of those legitimate long names. That consequence follows directly from the unconditional target refusal; I did not create and build that junction fixture. ([Target validation](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1769))

   **Change:** bound the **whole basename** to eight characters and constrain short-name characters. Add accepted cases such as `backup~2026` and `a b~1`, alongside refused cases such as `ABCDE~10` and `ABCD~100`. Those latter two already matched in my probes. Your “more than six characters before the tilde” premise cannot describe an eight-character basename containing a tilde and at least one digit.

3. **SUSPECTED — short aliases without a tilde can bypass the spelling policy.**  
   The helper admits `LONGFILE.TXT`; its short-name checks require a tilde. Microsoft explicitly documents assigning `longfile.txt` as the short name of `longfilename.txt`. Thus “does not resemble the generated tilde form” does not establish that a spelling is comparable. ([Helper](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1347), [Microsoft’s `setshortname` example](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/fsutil-file))

   **Uncompleted check:** assign that alias to a disposable directory and demonstrate the overlapping construction invocation. This read-only session cannot perform the setup.

   **Change:** resolve short aliases before identity-sensitive comparisons, or establish an enforceable filesystem restriction. Further tilde-pattern refinement cannot distinguish an assigned plain short alias from an ordinary name.

4. **CONFIRMED — the stream-root test still records a false independent refusal mechanism.**  
   Its docstring says `Test-Path` raises `ItemExistsNotSupportedError` and that this alone is enough to refuse the input. For `C:\Temp\pxd1:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION`, my direct probe returned **True on both hosts**. PowerShell 5.1 also printed that error and continued; PowerShell 7 printed no error. The script’s initial existence conditional therefore does not refuse this case. ([Docstring](C:/Temp/pxd1/evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:1952), [existence conditional](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1048))

   The positive test assertion correctly requires the colon diagnostic. **Change:** retain that assertion and describe the colon guard as the demonstrated refusal mechanism; remove “Either is enough.” ([Assertion](C:/Temp/pxd1/evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:1961))

5. **CONFIRMED — the doubled-backslash account and validation-order comment overclaim.**  
   “Every component check … was dead” is false. In an in-memory mutant restoring the doubled `Replace`, both hosts still refused `C:\Temp\mirror.` and `C:/Temp/ABC~1`. Splitting an unchanged string still produces a component, and existing forward slashes still split. The demonstrated failure is loss of interior backslash-separated component checking. ([Code comment](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1322), [debate account](C:/Temp/pxd1/docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/diff-debate.md:245))

6. **CONFIRMED — the category test’s claimed protection against a doubled fixture escape is false.**  
   Its comment says a doubled escape in the fixture “could never pass.” The current assertions accept the literal text `odd\u0890name.txt`, however. On both hosts, I verified that the formatter produces identical output for that literal ASCII string and for a name containing actual U+0890. Doubling the fixture escape can therefore stop exercising Unicode classification while satisfying the output assertions. ([Fixture claim](C:/Temp/pxd1/evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:2288), [assertions](C:/Temp/pxd1/evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:2303), [formatter](C:/Temp/pxd1/tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:928))

   The current fixture is correctly spelled. **Change:** assert that the seeded/read-back fixture contains `chr(0x0890)` and lacks the literal escape, then retain the positive rendering assertions. Apply the same fixture check to the neighboring control-character test. ([Neighboring fixture](C:/Temp/pxd1/evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:2261))

7. **CONFIRMED — several record details remain inaccurate.**  
   Item 98 and the debate account attribute the non-terminating-removal simulation to both hosts in round 3. The retained invocation substantiates PowerShell 7 only. I completed that simulation on both hosts this round and observed continuation to the intercepted `New-Item`, so the behavior now has both-host evidence, but the historical attribution needs correction. ([Item 98](C:/Temp/pxd1/BACKLOG.md:4281), [debate account](C:/Temp/pxd1/docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/diff-debate.md:223), [retained invocation](C:/Temp/pxd1/docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/diff-astra-r3-transcript.txt:1442))

   The live-measurement test also calls itself the **third** oracle instance beside the **second**, whereas its neighbor identifies itself as third and the debate correctly identifies the live-measurement case as fourth. ([Stale comment](C:/Temp/pxd1/evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:3089), [neighbor](C:/Temp/pxd1/evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py:3056), [record](C:/Temp/pxd1/docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/diff-debate.md:229))

   Finally, the round-1 account substitutes “control-character test” for the **runtime-category test** in the negative-only pair. The retained reply distinguishes that pair from the separate substring weakness.
